\displaystyle f({\bm{y}};K,m,r,l,{\mathbb{V}},{\bm{\theta}})

\displaystyle=f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}^{\mathbb{S}};K,m,{\bm{\alpha}})f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}^{{\mathbb{S}}^{c}};\check{{\bm{a}}}+{\bm{y}}^{\mathbb{R}}\check{{\bm{\beta}}},\check{{\bm{\Omega}}})

\displaystyle=f_{\text{${\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}$-eq}}({\bm{y}};K,m,{\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}},{\bm{\theta}}_{\text{${\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}$-eq}})

where
{\bm{\theta}}_{\text{${\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}$-eq}}=({\bm{\alpha}},\check{{\bm{a}}},\check{{\bm{\beta}}},\check{{\bm{\Omega}}})
.
From
Section

D.1
, the equivalent
{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}
densities for the two models, where the second model is denoted with
∗
) must be equal:

f_{\text{${\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}$-eq}}({\bm{y}};K,m,{\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}},{\bm{\theta}}_{\text{${\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}$-eq}})=f_{\text{${\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}$-eq}}({\bm{y}};K^{*},m^{*},{\mathbb{S}}^{*},{\mathbb{R}}^{*},{\bm{\theta}}_{\text{${\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}$-eq}}^{*})

By the identifiability of the
{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}
model
[
37
]
and three conditions in Theorem
1
, we deduce:
K=K^{*}
,
m=m^{*}
,
{\mathbb{S}}={\mathbb{S}}^{*}
,
{\mathbb{R}}={\mathbb{R}}^{*}
,
{\bm{\alpha}}={\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
,
\check{{\bm{a}}}=\check{{\bm{a}}}^{*}
,
\check{{\bm{\beta}}}=\check{{\bm{\beta}}}^{*}
, and
\check{{\bm{\Omega}}}=\check{{\bm{\Omega}}}^{*}
.

Since
{\mathbb{S}}={\mathbb{S}}^{*}
and
{\mathbb{S}}^{c}={\mathbb{S}}^{*c}
, supposed that, for contradiction, that
{\mathbb{U}}^{*}\cap{\mathbb{W}}\neq\emptyset
. Let
j\in{\mathbb{U}}^{*}\cap{\mathbb{W}}
. Since
j\in{\mathbb{W}}
, for all
q\in{\mathbb{U}}^{*}
the rows of
(\check{{\bm{\beta}}})_{qj}
are
{\bm{0}}^{T}
. Additionally, since
j\in{\mathbb{U}}^{*}
, there exists some
q\in{\mathbb{R}}*={\mathbb{R}}
such that
(\check{{\bm{\beta}}}^{*})_{qj}\neq{\bm{0}}^{T}
. However, we have established
\check{{\bm{\beta}}}=\check{{\bm{\beta}}}^{*}
. This is a contradiction:
(\check{{\bm{\beta}}})_{qj}
must be simultaneously
{\bm{0}}^{T}
and non-zero for all
q\in sR
. Therefore,
{\mathbb{U}}^{*}\cap{\mathbb{W}}=\emptyset
. By symmetry,
{\mathbb{U}}\cap{\mathbb{W}}^{*}=\emptyset
.
Given
{\mathbb{U}}\cup{\mathbb{W}}={\mathbb{U}}^{*}\cup{\mathbb{W}}^{*}
, the disjoint conditions imply
{\mathbb{U}}\subseteq{\mathbb{U}}^{*}
and
{\mathbb{U}}^{*}\subseteq{\mathbb{U}}
, hence
{\mathbb{U}}={\mathbb{U}}^{*}
. Consequently,
{\mathbb{W}}={\mathbb{W}}^{*}
. This establishes
{\mathbb{V}}={\mathbb{V}}^{*}
.
The parameter equalities
{\bm{a}}={\bm{a}}^{*},{\bm{\beta}}={\bm{\beta}}^{*},{\bm{\gamma}}={\bm{\gamma}}^{*},{\bm{\Omega}}={\bm{\Omega}}^{*},{\bm{\Gamma}}={\bm{\Gamma}}^{*}
and
r=r^{*},l=l^{*}
then follow directly from comparing the components of
\check{{\bm{a}}}=\check{{\bm{a}}}^{*}
,
\check{{\bm{\beta}}}=\check{{\bm{\beta}}}^{*}
,
\check{{\bm{\Omega}}}=\check{{\bm{\Omega}}}^{*}
, condition 3 in Theorem
1
. Thus, the SRUW parameters under MAR are identifiable.

Now, we extend this to the full model which includes the MNARz mechanism parameters
{\bm{\psi}}=\{{\psi}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
. The full set of parameters for the MNARz-SRUW model is
({\bm{\theta}},{\bm{\psi}})
. Suppose that the two models
{\mathcal{M}}_{A}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{B}
produce the same observed-data likelihood for all observed data
({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n})
:

L_{\text{MNARz-SRUW}}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n};{\bm{\theta}}_{A}^{\text{data}},{\bm{\psi}}_{A}^{\text{MNARz}})=L_{\text{MNARz-SRUW}}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n};{\bm{\theta}}_{B}^{\text{data}},{\bm{\psi}}_{B}^{\text{MNARz}})\quad\forall({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n}),

By Theorem
5
, the equality of observed-data likelihoods under MNARz-SRUW:

L_{\text{MNARz-SRUW}}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n};{\bm{\theta}}_{A}^{\text{data}},{\bm{\psi}}_{A}^{\text{MNARz}})=L_{\text{MNARz-SRUW}}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n};{\bm{\theta}}_{B}^{\text{data}},{\bm{\psi}}_{B}^{\text{MNARz}})

for all
({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n})
implies the equality of the likelihoods for the augmented observed data
\tilde{{\bm{y}}}_{n}^{o}=({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n})
under the MAR interpretation where
f({\bm{c}}_{n}|z_{nk}=1;{\psi}_{k})
is treated as part of the component density:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K_{A}}({\pi}_{A})_{k}f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk}=1;({\theta}_{A}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k})f({\bm{c}}_{n}|z_{nk}=1;({\psi}_{A})_{k})

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K_{B}}({\pi}_{B})_{k^{\prime}}f_{k^{\prime}}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk^{\prime}}=1;({\theta}_{B}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k^{\prime}})f({\bm{c}}_{n}|z_{nk^{\prime}}=1;({\psi}_{B})_{k^{\prime}})

(16)

for all
({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n})
. Let
g_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n};({\theta}_{A})_{k},({\psi}_{A})_{k})=f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk}=1;({\theta}_{A}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k})f({\bm{c}}_{n}|z_{nk}=1;({\psi}_{A})_{k})
. This means two finite mixture models on the augmented data
({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n})
are identical. If
\sum_{k=1}^{K_{A}}({\pi}_{A})_{k}g_{k}(\cdot)=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K_{B}}({\pi}_{B})_{k^{\prime}}g^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}(\cdot)
, and the family of component densities
\{g_{k}\}
(and
\{g^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\}
) is identifiable and satisfies certain linear independence conditions (
[
68
]
,
[
2
]
), then
K_{A}=K_{B}=K
, and after a permutation of labels,
({\pi}_{A})_{k}=({\pi}_{B})_{k}
and
g_{k}(\cdot)=g^{\prime}_{k}(\cdot)
for all
k
. Then, we need to ensure the family of component densities
h_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n};{\theta}_{k},{\psi}_{k})=f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk}=1;{\theta}_{k})f({\bm{c}}_{n}|z_{nk}=1;{\psi}_{k})
meets such conditions. Hence, we further assume:

1.

The parameter sets
{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{SRUW}}
and
{\bm{\psi}}^{\text{MNARz}}
are functionally independent

2.

The support of
{\bm{y}}_{n}
and
{\bm{c}}_{n}
is rich enough such that equalities holding for all
({\bm{y}}_{n},{\bm{c}}_{n})
imply functional equalities. This implies there is
sufficient variability
of
{\bm{y}}_{n}
and
{\bm{c}}_{n}
.

These assumptions hold because
{\psi}
parametrizes only
f({\bm{c}}_{n}|{\bm{z}})
and
\theta
only
f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|{\bm{z}})
; hence there is no functional overlap. Applying those assumptions to
16
, we conclude that
K_{A}=K_{B}=K
, and up to a permutation of labels:

({\pi}_{A})_{k}=({\pi}_{B})_{k}\quad\text{for all }k,

(17)

and

\begin{gathered}f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk}=1;({\theta}_{A}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k})f({\bm{c}}_{n}|z_{nk}=1;({\psi}_{A})_{k})\\
=\\
f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk}=1;({\theta}_{B}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k})f({\bm{c}}_{n}|z_{nk}=1;({\psi}_{B})_{k})\\
\end{gathered}

(18)

for all
({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n})
and for each
k=1,\dots,K
.

Now we need to deduce that
({\theta}_{A}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k}=({\theta}_{B}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k}
and
({\psi}_{A})_{k}=({\psi}_{B})_{k}
.

Equation

18
can be written as:

A_{1}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})B_{1}({\bm{c}}_{n})=A_{2}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})B_{2}({\bm{c}}_{n})

where
A_{1}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})=f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk}=1;({\theta}_{A}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k})
,
B_{1}({\bm{c}}_{n})=f({\bm{c}}_{n}|z_{nk}=1;({\psi}_{A})_{k})
. By defined assumptions, this equality holding for all
{\bm{y}}_{n}^{o},{\bm{c}}_{n}
implies a relationship between these functions. Since
{\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}
and
{\bm{c}}_{n}
can vary independently
1
1

1

Since, for each component
k
, the joint component density factorizes as
f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk}=1;{\theta}_{k})\,f({\bm{c}}_{n}|z_{nk}=1;{\psi}_{k})
and both factors are positive on sets of positive measure, pick
c_{0}
in that common positive-measure set; integrating
A_{1}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})B_{1}(c_{0})=A_{2}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})B_{2}(c_{0})
over
{\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}
gives
B_{1}(c_{0})=B_{2}(c_{0})
, hence
A_{1}=A_{2}
, and then
B_{1}=B_{2}
.

and the parameters
({\theta}_{A})_{k},({\psi}_{A})_{k}
are functionally independent, if
\int A_{1}B_{1}d{\bm{y}}^{o}d{\bm{c}}=\int A_{2}B_{2}d{\bm{y}}^{o}d{\bm{c}}=1
, and
A_{1}B_{1}=A_{2}B_{2}
pointwise, and assuming
A_{1},B_{1},A_{2},B_{2}
are non-zero almost everywhere on their support:
Pick a
{\bm{c}}_{n}^{0}
such that
f({\bm{c}}_{n}^{0}|k;({\psi}_{A})_{k})\neq 0
and
f({\bm{c}}_{n}^{0}|k;({\psi}_{B})_{k})\neq 0
. Then for this fixed
{\bm{c}}_{n}^{0}
:

f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk}=1;({\theta}_{A}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k})\cdot C_{A}=f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk}=1;({\theta}_{B}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k})\cdot C_{B}

where
C_{A}=f({\bm{c}}_{n}^{0}|k;({\psi}_{A})_{k})
and
C_{B}=f({\bm{c}}_{n}^{0}|k;({\psi}_{B})_{k})
. Since both
f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|\dots)
are conditional densities , this implies
C_{A}=C_{B}
and thus

f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk}=1;({\theta}_{A}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k})=f_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}|z_{nk}=1;({\theta}_{B}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k})\quad\text{for all }{\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}.

By identifiability of SRUW under MAR, this implies that the structural SRUW parameters
(m_{A},r_{A},l_{A},{\mathbb{V}}_{A})
must equal
(m_{B},r_{B},l_{B},{\mathbb{V}}_{B})
and the parameters
({\theta}_{A}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k}=({\theta}_{B}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k}
.

Since
C_{A}=C_{B}
means
f({\bm{c}}_{n}^{0}|k;({\psi}_{A})_{k})=f({\bm{c}}_{n}^{0}|k;({\psi}_{B})_{k})
, and this must hold for all
{\bm{c}}_{n}^{0}
(by choosing different fixed
{\bm{c}}_{n}^{0}
or by varying
{\bm{y}}_{n}^{o}
first), this implies:

f({\bm{c}}_{n}|k;({\psi}_{A})_{k})=f({\bm{c}}_{n}|k;({\psi}_{B})_{k})\quad\text{for all }{\bm{c}}_{n}.

By idenitifiability of MNARz, this implies
({\psi}_{A})_{k}=({\psi}_{B})_{k}
.

Thus, we have
K_{A}=K_{B}=K
, and up to a common permutation of labels for
k=1,\dots,K
:

\displaystyle({\pi}_{A})_{k}=({\pi}_{B})_{k}

\displaystyle(m_{A},r_{A},l_{A},{\mathbb{V}}_{A})=(m_{B},r_{B},l_{B},{\mathbb{V}}_{B})

\displaystyle({\theta}_{A}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k}=({\theta}_{B}^{\text{SRUW}})_{k}

\displaystyle({\psi}_{A})_{k}=({\psi}_{B})_{k}

This implies the full set of model specifications and parameters for
{\mathcal{M}}_{A}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{B}
is identical. Therefore, the SRUW model under the specified MNARz mechanism is identifiable.
∎

D.2
Proof of Theorem
2

Proof Sketch.

Suppose that the observations are from a parametric model
{\mathcal{P}}_{\bm{\Theta}}=\{f(\cdot;{\bm{\theta}}):{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}\}
, and assume that the distributions in
{\mathcal{P}}_{\bm{\Theta}}
are dominated by a common
\sigma
-finite measure
\upsilon
with respect to which they have probability density functions
f(\cdot;{\bm{\theta}})
.

Now, we need to prove the consistency of the sample KL, which will lead to the consistency of the BIC criterion in both the
{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}
and
{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}{\mathbb{U}}{\mathbb{W}}
models under the MAR mechanism. First, we prove the consistency of the sample KL in the
{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}
model:

Proposition 2
.

Under Assumption
1
and
2
, for all
({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})
,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln\left(\frac{h({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})}{f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})}\right)\underset{n\to\infty}{\overset{P}{\to}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}\Bigl[h,f(\cdot;{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr].

Proof.

Let
{\mathbb{O}}\subset\{1,\dots,D\}
denote the indices of the observed components.

Recall that the full covariance matrix for the component
k
,
{\bm{\Delta}}_{k}
, is built as:

{\Delta}_{k,jl}=\begin{cases}\Sigma_{k,jl},&j,l\in{\mathbb{S}},\\
({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}{\bm{\Lambda}})_{jl},&j\in{\mathbb{S}},\,l\in{\mathbb{S}}^{c},\\
({\bm{\Lambda}}^{T}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k})_{jl},&j\in{\mathbb{S}}^{c},\,l\in{\mathbb{S}},\\
({\bm{\Omega}}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{T}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}{\bm{\Lambda}})_{jl},&j,l\in{\mathbb{S}}^{c}.\end{cases}

For indices
j,l\in{\mathbb{S}}
:
As before,

{\Delta}_{k,jl}=\Sigma_{k,jl}.

Thus, eigenvalues of
\Delta_{k,{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{S}}}
are in
[s_{m},s_{M}]
.

For
j\in{\mathbb{S}},\,l\in{\mathbb{S}}^{c}
:

{\Delta}_{k,jl}=({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}{\bm{\Lambda}})_{jl}.

The norm of the product satisfies

\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}{\bm{\Lambda}}\rVert\leq\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}\rVert\cdot\lVert{\bm{\Lambda}}\rVert.

Since
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\lvert{\mathbb{S}}\rvert}
,

\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}\rVert=\lambda_{\max}({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k})\leq s_{M}.

Given
{\bm{\beta}}\in\mathcal{B}(\rho,\lvert{\mathbb{R}}\rvert,\lvert{\mathbb{S}}^{c}\rvert)
,

\lVert{\bm{\Lambda}}\rVert\leq\rho.

Thus,

\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}{\bm{\Lambda}}\rVert\leq s_{M}\rho.

This bounds the norm of the
({\mathbb{S}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{c})
block of
{\bm{\Delta}}_{k}
.

For
j,l\in{\mathbb{S}}^{c}
:

{\Delta}_{k,jl}=({\bm{\Omega}}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{T}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}{\bm{\Lambda}})_{jl}.

We can bound the eigenvalues of this sum using the spectral norm:

\lVert{\bm{\Omega}}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{T}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}{\bm{\Lambda}}\rVert\leq\lVert{\bm{\Omega}}\rVert+\lVert{\bm{\Lambda}}^{T}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}{\bm{\Lambda}}\rVert.

Since
{\bm{\Omega}}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\lvert{\mathbb{S}}^{c}\rvert}
,

\lVert{\bm{\Omega}}\rVert=\lambda_{\max}({\bm{\Omega}})\leq s_{M}.

Next,

\lVert{\bm{\Lambda}}^{T}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}{\bm{\Lambda}}\rVert\leq\lVert{\bm{\Lambda}}^{T}\rVert\cdot\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}\rVert\cdot\lVert{\bm{\Lambda}}\rVert=\lVert{\bm{\Lambda}}\rVert^{2}\cdot\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}\rVert\leq\rho^{2}s_{M}.

Thus,

\lVert{\bm{\Omega}}+{\bm{\Lambda}}^{T}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}{\bm{\Lambda}}\rVert\leq s_{M}+\rho^{2}s_{M}=s_{M}(1+\rho^{2}).

Given the above bounds, there exist refined constants
\tilde{s}_{m}>0
and

\tilde{s}_{M}=\max\{s_{M}(1+\rho^{2}),\ s_{M}\rho,\ s_{M}\}=s_{M}(1+\rho^{2})

such that

\tilde{s}_{m}I\preceq{\bm{\Delta}}_{k}\preceq\tilde{s}_{M}I.

Note that
\tilde{s}_{M}
will depend on the sizes of the blocks, and it is finite being dependent on
s_{M}
,
\rho
, and the structure of
{\bm{\Omega}}
,
{\bm{\beta}}
, and
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}
.

Since
{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}
is a principal submatrix of
{\bm{\Delta}}_{k}
and
{\bm{\Delta}}_{k}
is a symmetric matrix, by the interlacing theorem:

\tilde{s}_{m}\leq\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo})\leq\lambda_{\max}({\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo})\leq\tilde{s}_{M}.

Thus,

\tilde{s}_{m}\,I_{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}\preceq{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}\preceq\tilde{s}_{M}\,I_{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert},

and

({\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo})^{-1}\preceq\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{m}}\,I_{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}.

We are now moving to the main proof. By hypothesis, the true observed-data density is given by

h({\bm{y}}^{o})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}\,\Phi\Bigl({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\nu}}^{*}_{k,o},\Delta^{*}_{k,oo}\Bigr)

and if we can show that

\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{y}}^{o}}\left[|\ln h({\bm{y}}^{o})|\right]<\infty.

then by the LLN,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln\bigl(h({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})\bigr)\underset{n\to\infty}{\overset{P}{\to}}\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{y}}^{o}}{\bigl[\ln h({\bm{y}}^{o})\bigr]}.

Moreover, the observed-data likelihood under the model is

f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}\,\Phi\Bigl({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o},{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}\Bigr).

and we later show in Proposition
3
that

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln\bigl(f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\bigr)\underset{n\to\infty}{\overset{P}{\to}}\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{y}}^{o}}{\bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\bigr]}.

To do so, we must verify that the class of functions

\mathcal{F}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}=\Bigl\{{\bm{y}}^{o}\mapsto\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}):{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}\Bigr\}

satisfies the conditions for uniform convergence under Assumption
2
. In particular, by Assumption
2
, the parameter space
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}
is compact, and for every
{\bm{y}}^{o}\in\mathds{R}^{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}
the mapping

{\bm{\theta}}\mapsto\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})

is continuous. Next, we verify that there is an
h
-integrable envelope function
F\in\mathcal{F}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}
. Recalling that for

{\bm{y}}^{o}\in\mathds{R}^{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}
, the Gaussian component density is

\Phi\Big({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o},{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}\Big)=(2\pi)^{-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}}|{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\Big(-\tfrac{1}{2}({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o})^{T}{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}^{-1}({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o})\Big).

We can derive the bounds for this as follows.

For the upper bound, since
\lambda_{\min}({\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo})\geq\tilde{s}_{m}
,

|{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\leq(\tilde{s}_{m})^{-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}}.

With
\exp(-\cdot)\leq 1
and
{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}
is positive definite

\Phi\Big({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o},{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}\Big)\leq(2\pi)^{-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}}(\tilde{s}_{m})^{-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}}.

Summing over
k
and using
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}=1
,

f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\leq(2\pi\tilde{s}_{m})^{-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}}.

Taking logarithms:

\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\leq-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{m}).

For the lower bound, for each
k
,

\ln\Phi\Big({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o},{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}\Big)=-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi)-\frac{1}{2}\ln|{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}|-\frac{1}{2}({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o})^{T}{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}^{-1}({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o}).

Since
\lambda_{\max}({\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo})\leq\tilde{s}_{M}
,

\ln|{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}|\leq\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert\ln(\tilde{s}_{M}),

and
{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}^{-1}\preceq\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{m}}I
,

({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o})^{T}{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}^{-1}({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o})\leq\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o}\|^{2}}{\tilde{s}_{m}}.

Using
\|{\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o}\|^{2}\leq 2(\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+\|{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o}\|^{2})
,

({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o})^{T}{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}^{-1}({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o})\leq\frac{2(\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+\|{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o}\|^{2})}{\tilde{s}_{m}}.

Given the construction of the mean vector
{\bm{\nu}}_{k}
for each component
k
:

\nu_{kj}=\begin{cases}\mu_{kj},&\text{if }j\in{\mathbb{S}},\\[6.0pt]
(a+{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\Lambda)_{j},&\text{if }j\in{\mathbb{S}}^{c},\end{cases}

and knowing that
a
belongs to the closed ball
{\mathcal{B}}(\rho,1,\lvert{\mathbb{S}}^{c}\rvert)
-a set of
1\times\lvert{\mathbb{S}}^{c}\rvert
matrices with norm bounded by
\rho
-we seek to derive a uniform bound for
{\bm{\nu}}_{k}
. For indices
j\in{\mathbb{S}}
,
\nu_{kj}=\mu_{kj}
, and since the parameter space
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{V}
is compact, the cluster means
\mu_{kj}
are bounded by some constant
\eta>0
. For indices
j\in{\mathbb{S}}^{c}
, we have
\nu_{kj}=(a+{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\Lambda)_{j}=a_{j}+({\bm{\mu}}_{k}\Lambda)_{j}
. Using the elementary inequality
(u+v)^{2}\leq 2(u^{2}+v^{2})
, it follows that

\lVert\nu_{kj}\rVert^{2}\leq 2\Big(\lVert a_{j}\rVert^{2}+\lVert({\bm{\mu}}_{k}\Lambda)_{j}\rVert^{2}\Big).

Given that
a
lies in
{\mathcal{B}}(\rho,1,\lvert{\mathbb{S}}^{c}\rvert)
,
\lVert a_{j}\rVert^{2}\leq\rho^{2}
for each
j\in{\mathbb{S}}^{c}
. In addition, the term
({\bm{\mu}}_{k}\Lambda)_{j}
can be bounded by
\|{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\|_{2}\|\Lambda_{\cdot j}\|_{2}
, where
\Lambda_{\cdot j}
is the
j
-th column of
\Lambda
. With
{\bm{\mu}}_{k}
belonging to a compact set
{\mathcal{B}}(\eta,\lvert{\mathbb{S}}\rvert)
,
\lVert{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\rVert^{2}\leq\eta^{2}
, and because
\Lambda
is derived from bounded parameters (including
{\bm{\beta}}
with norm being bounded by
\rho^{2}
), each column
\Lambda_{\cdot j}
is bounded in norm by
\rho^{2}
. Consequently,
|({\bm{\mu}}_{k}\Lambda)_{j}|\leq\rho^{2}+\eta^{2}\rho^{2}=\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})
for each
j\in{\mathbb{S}}^{c}
. Combining these results, for any
j\in{\mathbb{S}}^{c}
,

\lVert\nu_{kj}\rVert^{2}\leq 2\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2}).

Thus, all entries of
{\bm{\nu}}_{k}
, irrespective of whether they correspond to indices in
{\mathbb{S}}
or
{\mathbb{S}}^{c}
, are bounded by a constant that depends on
\eta
,
\rho
. Hence,

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\nu}}_{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j}\|{\bm{\nu}}_{k,j}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j}2\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})

\displaystyle=2D\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})

uniformly for all
k
.

Consider again the Gaussian density for the observed variables

\ln\Phi\Big({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o},{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}\Big)=-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi)-\frac{1}{2}\ln|{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}|-\frac{1}{2}({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o})^{T}{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}^{-1}({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o}).

To control the quadratic term in the exponent, we note that

\|{\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o}\|^{2}\leq 2\Big(\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+\|{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o}\|^{2}\Big).

Given that
\|{\bm{\nu}}_{k}\|=\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{O}}}\lVert\nu_{kj}\rVert^{2}\leq 2\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})
is uniformly bounded, it follows that

\|{\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o}\|^{2}\leq 2\Big(\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+2\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})\Big).

This bound ensures that the quadratic form
({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o})^{T}{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}^{-1}({\bm{y}}^{o}-{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o})
remains finite. The uniform boundedness of
{\bm{\nu}}_{k}
thus allows us to assert the existence of an integrable envelope function
F({\bm{y}}^{o})
that uniformly bounds
|\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})|
for all
{\bm{\theta}}
within the compact parameter space
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{V}
.

Thus,

\ln\Phi\Big({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o},{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}\Big)\geq-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi)-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(\tilde{s}_{M})-\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{2(\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+2\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2}))}{\tilde{s}_{m}}.

Simplifying,

\ln\Phi\Big({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o},{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}\Big)\geq-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{M})-\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+2\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2}))}{\tilde{s}_{m}}.

Using Jensen’s inequality over the mixture:

\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\geq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}\Bigg(-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{M})-\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+2\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2}))}{\tilde{s}_{m}}\Bigg).

Since
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}=1
,

\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\geq-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{M})-\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+2\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2}))}{\tilde{s}_{m}}.

Combining the refined upper and lower bounds:

-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{M})-\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+2\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2}))}{\tilde{s}_{m}}\leq\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\leq-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{m}).

As a final note, these bounds also rely on the eigenvalue constraints on
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}
,
{\bm{\Omega}}
, and the norm constraint on
{\bm{\beta}}
.

Now we prove that the envelope function
F
, which is related to the upper and lower bounds above, is
h
-integrable. The true density
h({\bm{y}})
corresponds to a Gaussian mixture model
f({\bm{y}};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}}_{0},{\mathbb{R}}_{0})})
with parameters in a compact set. The observed-data density for
{\bm{y}}^{o}
, obtained by marginalizing over the missing components, is given by

h({\bm{y}}^{o})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}\,\Phi\Big({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\nu}}^{*}_{k,o},\,\Delta^{*}_{k,oo}\Big).

To verify that the envelope function
F
is
h
-integrable, we need to show

\int\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}<\infty.

We proceed by examining the second moment of the observed components. First, observe that

\displaystyle\int\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}\int\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}\,\Phi\Big({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\nu}}^{*}_{k,o},\,\Delta^{*}_{k,oo}\Big)d{\bm{y}}^{o}.

\displaystyle\leq 2\|{\bm{\nu}}^{*}_{k,o}\|^{2}+2\operatorname{tr}(\Delta^{*}_{k,oo}).

by using Lemma. By the compactness of the parameter space
{\bm{\Theta}}_{\mathbb{V}}
and since
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\ \pi_{k}=1
and the bounds derived earlier, we have

\int\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}\leq\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})+2s_{M}|{\mathbb{S}}_{0,o}|<\infty

Therefore,
F
is h-integrable, i.e.,

\int F({\bm{y}}^{o})h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}<\infty,

since
\int\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}<\infty
.

Finally, because
|\ln(h({\bm{y}}^{o}))|\leq F({\bm{y}}^{o})
, it implies

\mathbb{E}[|\ln(h({\bm{y}}^{o}))|]=\int|\ln(h({\bm{y}}^{o}))|h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}\leq\int F({\bm{y}}^{o})h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}<\infty.

Hence, the envelope function
F
is
h
-integrable and
\mathbb{E}[|\ln(h({\bm{y}}^{o}))|]<\infty
. Thus, we can apply the law of large numbers to obtain the consistency of the sample KL divergence.
∎

Proposition 3
.

Assume that

1.

({\bm{y}}_{1}^{o},\ldots,{\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})
are i.i.d. observed vectors with unknown density
h
.

2.

{\bm{\Theta}}
is a compact metric space.

3.

{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}\mapsto\ln[f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})]
is continuous for every
{\bm{y}}^{o}\in\mathds{R}^{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}
.

4.

F
is an envelope function of
\mathcal{F}:=\{\ln[f(\cdot;{\bm{\theta}})];\ {\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}\}
which is
h
-integrable.

5.

{\bm{\theta}}^{*}=\operatorname*{argmax}_{{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}[h,f(\cdot;{\bm{\theta}})]

6.

\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}=\operatorname*{argmax}_{{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\theta}})
.

Then, as
n\to\infty
,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\underset{n\to\infty}{\overset{P}{\to}}\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{y}}^{o}}{\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]}.

Proof.

We consider the following inequality:

\Biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Biggr|

\leq\Biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]\Biggr|+\sup_{{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}}\Biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Bigr]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Biggr|.

By the definition of
{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]\geq 0.

Note that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})}_{I}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})}_{II}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})+\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})}_{III}\Bigl]

For term
II
, sine
\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}
maximizes the empirical log-likelihood,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln(f(X_{n}\lvert\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln(f(X_{n}\lvert{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}),

which implies that
II\leq 0
. Therefore,

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]\leq\lvert I\rvert+\lvert III\rvert.

Moreover, since both
\lvert I\rvert\text{ and }\lvert III\rvert
are bounded by the uniform deviation:

\displaystyle\lvert I\rvert\leq\sup_{{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}}\Biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Bigr]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Biggr|

and

\displaystyle\lvert II\rvert\leq\sup_{{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}}\Biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Bigr]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Biggr|

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\lvert I\rvert+\lvert III\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}}\Biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Bigr]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Biggr|.

Hence, the left-hand side is bounded by three times the uniform deviation:

\Biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Biggr|\leq 3\sup_{{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}}\Biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Bigr]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Biggr|.

By the argument in the Proposition
2
-namely, using the compactness of
{\bm{\Theta}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}
, the continuity of
{\bm{\theta}}\mapsto\ln f(\cdot;{\bm{\theta}})
, and the existence of an
h
-integrable envelope
F
-the class
\mathcal{F}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}
is P-Glivenko-Cantelli by applying Example 19.8 in van der Vaart (1998) to conclude on the finiteness of bracketing numbers of
\mathcal{F}
under the assumptions. In particular,

\sup_{{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})}}\Biggl|\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Bigr]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Biggr|\underset{n\to\infty}{\overset{P}{\to}}0.

Therefore,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\underset{n\to\infty}{\overset{P}{\to}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}})})\Bigr].

which concludes the proof of Proposition
2
.
∎

Now, we prove the consistency of the sample KL in the
{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}{\mathbb{U}}{\mathbb{W}}
model:

Proposition 4
.

Under Assumption
4
and
5
, for all
{\mathbb{V}}
,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln\left(\frac{h({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})}{f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{{\mathbb{V}}})}\right)\underset{n\to\infty}{\overset{P}{\to}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}\Bigl[h,f(\cdot;{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{{\mathbb{V}}})\Bigr].

Proof.

We carry the proof in the same manner as in the
{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}
model. Let
{\mathbb{V}}=({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{U}},{\mathbb{W}})
and
{\mathbb{O}}\subset\{1,\dots,D\}
denotes the observed variables. We still want to apply the Proposition
3
with the new family

\mathcal{F}_{({\mathbb{V}})}\coloneqq\{\ln[f(\cdot\lvert{\bm{\theta}})];\,{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{V}}}\}

similarly to the proof of Proposition
2
to achieve

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln\bigl(f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{{\mathbb{V}}})\bigr)\underset{n\to\infty}{\overset{P}{\to}}\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{y}}^{o}}{\bigl[\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{{\mathbb{V}}})\bigr]}.

To do so, we must initially verify that the class of functions

\mathcal{F}_{({\mathbb{V}})}=\Bigl\{{\bm{y}}^{o}\mapsto\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}):{\bm{\theta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{V}}}\Bigr\}

satisfies the conditions for uniform convergence under Assumption
5
. In particular, by Assumption
5
, the parameter space
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{V}}}
is compact, and for every
{\bm{y}}^{o}\in\mathds{R}^{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}
the mapping

{\bm{\theta}}\mapsto\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})

is continuous. Next, we verify that there is an
h
-integrable envelope function
F\in\mathcal{F}_{({\mathbb{V}})}
. Recalling that for

{\bm{y}}^{o}\in\mathds{R}^{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}
and a given component
k
, the current Gaussian component density is

\Phi\Big({\bm{y}}^{o};\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o},\tilde{{\bm{\Delta}}}_{k,oo}\Big)=(2\pi)^{-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}}|\tilde{{\bm{\Delta}}}_{k,oo}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\exp\Big(-\tfrac{1}{2}({\bm{y}}^{o}-\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o})^{T}\tilde{{\bm{\Delta}}}_{k,oo}^{-1}({\bm{y}}^{o}-\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o})\Big).

We will bound the density function as usual. From Proposition
2
, we know that there exists
\tilde{s}_{m}>0
and
\tilde{s}^{\prime}_{M}=\max\{s_{M}(1+\rho^{2}),s_{M}\rho,s_{M}\}=s_{M}(1+\rho^{2})
such that

\tilde{s}_{m}I\preceq{\bm{\Delta}}_{k}\preceq\tilde{s}_{M}I.

Thus,

\tilde{s}_{m}\,I_{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}\preceq{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}\preceq\tilde{s}_{M}\,I_{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert},

and

({\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo})^{-1}\preceq\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{m}}\,I_{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}.

Given that
{\bm{\Gamma}}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\lvert{\mathbb{W}}\rvert}
and is independent of any relevant variables, we have that the principal sub-matrix
{\bm{\Gamma}}_{oo}
is bounded by
s_{M}

\lambda_{\max}({\bm{\Gamma}}_{oo})\leq s_{M}.

Since
\tilde{{\bm{\Delta}}}_{k,oo}
is block-diagonal, its eigenvalues are the union of the eigenvalues of
{\bm{\Delta}}_{k,oo}
and
{\bm{\Gamma}}_{oo}
. Hence,

\lambda_{\max}(\tilde{{\bm{\Delta}}}_{k,oo})\leq\max\{s_{M}(1+\rho^{2}),\,s_{M}\}=s_{M}(1+\rho^{2}).

We define the upper bound by

\tilde{s}_{M}:=s_{M}(1+\rho^{2}).

The lower bound of the structured block, together with the lower bound on the independent block, implies that the covariance
\tilde{{\bm{\Delta}}}_{k,oo}
satisfies

\tilde{s}_{m}\,I\preceq\tilde{{\bm{\Delta}}}_{k,oo}\preceq\tilde{s}_{M}\,I.

Because
\tilde{{\bm{\Delta}}}_{k,oo}^{-1}\preceq\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{m}}I
, it follows that

({\bm{y}}^{o}-\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o})^{T}\tilde{{\bm{\Delta}}}_{k,oo}^{-1}({\bm{y}}^{o}-\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o})\leq\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}-\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o}\|^{2}}{\tilde{s}_{m}}.

Using the elementary inequality

\|{\bm{y}}^{o}-\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o}\|^{2}\leq 2\Bigl(\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+\|\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o}\|^{2}\Bigr),

we obtain

({\bm{y}}^{o}-\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o})^{T}\tilde{{\bm{\Delta}}}_{k,oo}^{-1}({\bm{y}}^{o}-\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o})\leq\frac{2\Bigl(\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+\|\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o}\|^{2}\Bigr)}{\tilde{s}_{m}}.

Now we process the bound for
\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o}
. Recalling that if we denote
D_{{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{U}}}
denote the number of coordinates in the structured part (i.e.
{\mathbb{S}}\cup{\mathbb{U}}
); then

\|{\bm{\nu}}_{k,o}\|^{2}=\sum_{j\in o}\lVert\nu_{kj}\rVert^{2}\leq D_{{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{U}},o}\,\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})

is uniformly bounded and
\lVert\gamma_{o}\rVert^{2}\leq\eta^{2}
since
\gamma
belongs to the compact set
{\mathcal{B}}(\eta,\lvert{\mathbb{W}}\rvert)
, it follows that

\|\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o}\|^{2}\leq D_{{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{U}},o}\rho^{2}(1+\eta)^{2}+\eta^{2},

We deduce that

\ln\Phi\Bigl({\bm{y}}^{o};\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o},\tilde{{\bm{\Delta}}}_{k,oo}\Bigr)\geq-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln\bigl(2\pi\,\tilde{s}_{M}\bigr)-\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+D_{{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{U}},o}\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})+\eta^{2}}{\tilde{s}_{m}}.

Using Jensen’s inequality over the mixture and
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}=1
, we have

\displaystyle\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}\Bigg(-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{M})-\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+D_{{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{U}},o}\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})+\eta^{2}}{\tilde{s}_{m}}\Bigg).

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{M})-\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+D_{{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{U}},o}\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})+\eta^{2}}{\tilde{s}_{m}}

Therefore, each family’s member in
\mathcal{F}_{({\mathbb{V}})}
is bounded by

-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{M})-\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+D_{{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{U}},o}\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})+\eta^{2}}{\tilde{s}_{m}}\leq\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}}_{({\mathbb{V}})})\leq-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{m}).

Now we prove that
F
is
h
-integrable. The true observed-data density under the
{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}{\mathbb{U}}{\mathbb{W}}
model is given by

h({\bm{y}}^{o})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\pi_{k}\,\Phi\Bigl({\bm{y}}^{o};\tilde{{\bm{\nu}}}_{k,o}^{*},\,\tilde{\Delta}^{*}_{k,oo}\Bigr),

where the “
*
” indicates that the parameters are the true ones and the density is that of a Gaussian mixture with parameters in a compact set. In our derivation we have shown that, for any
{\bm{\theta}}
in the family
\mathcal{F}_{({\mathbb{V}})}
, the log-density satisfies

-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{M})-\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+D_{{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{U}},o}\,\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})+\eta^{2}}{\tilde{s}_{m}}\leq\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\leq-\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln(2\pi\tilde{s}_{m}).

In other words, every function
\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})
is bounded in absolute value by

F({\bm{y}}^{o})=\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln\bigl(2\pi\tilde{s}_{M}\bigr)+\frac{\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}+D_{{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{U}},o}\,\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})+\eta^{2}}{\tilde{s}_{m}}.

Hence, for all
{\bm{\theta}}
in the compact parameter space,

\Bigl|\ln f({\bm{y}}^{o};{\bm{\theta}})\Bigr|\leq F({\bm{y}}^{o}).

To verify that
F
is
h
-integrable, we must show that

\int F({\bm{y}}^{o})\,h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}<\infty.

Since the envelope function
F
is of the form

F({\bm{y}}^{o})=C_{0}+\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{m}}\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2},

with

C_{0}=\frac{\lvert{\mathbb{O}}\rvert}{2}\ln\bigl(2\pi\tilde{s}_{M}\bigr)+\frac{D_{{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{U}},o}\,\rho^{2}(1+\eta^{2})+\eta^{2}}{\tilde{s}_{m}},

it is clear that

\int F({\bm{y}}^{o})\,h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}\leq C_{0}+\frac{1}{\tilde{s}_{m}}\int\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}\,h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}.

Because
h({\bm{y}}^{o})
is a Gaussian mixture with parameters in a compact set, standard properties of Gaussian mixtures guarantee that the second moment is finite, that is,

\int\|{\bm{y}}^{o}\|^{2}\,h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}<\infty.

Thus,

\int F({\bm{y}}^{o})\,h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}<\infty.

Finally, since for every
{\bm{y}}^{o}
we have

\bigl|\ln h({\bm{y}}^{o})\bigr|\leq F({\bm{y}}^{o}),

It follows that

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[|\ln h({\bm{y}}^{o})|\Bigr]=\int|\ln h({\bm{y}}^{o})|\,h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}\leq\int F({\bm{y}}^{o})\,h({\bm{y}}^{o})\,d{\bm{y}}^{o}<\infty.

Therefore, the envelope function
F
is
h
-integrable and
\mathbb{E}[|\ln(h({\bm{y}}^{o}))|]<\infty
, and consequently, we can apply the law of large numbers and uniform convergence to conclude the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2
.

Define the BIC score of a variable partition
{\mathbb{V}}=({\mathbb{S}},{\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{U}},{\mathbb{W}})
by

\mbox{BIC}({\mathbb{V}})=2\ell_{N}\bigl(\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{{\mathbb{V}}}\bigr)-\Xi_{({\mathbb{V}})}\log N,

where
\Xi_{({\mathbb{V}})}
is the number of free parameters. Let
{\mathbb{V}}_{0}
be the true partition and set
\Delta\mbox{BIC}({\mathbb{V}})=\mbox{BIC}({\mathbb{V}}_{0})-\mbox{BIC}({\mathbb{V}})
.

Let
{\mathbb{V}}_{1}=\bigl\{{\mathbb{V}}\neq{\mathbb{V}}_{0}:D_{\mathrm{KL}}[h,f(\cdot;{\bm{\theta}}_{{\mathbb{V}}}^{\star})]>0\bigr\}
. The Identifiability Theorem
1
implies every
{\mathbb{V}}\neq{\mathbb{V}}_{0}
belongs to
{\mathbb{V}}_{1}
. For
{\mathbb{V}}\in{\mathbb{V}}_{1}
,

\Delta\mbox{BIC}({\mathbb{V}})=2N\Biggl[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln\left(\frac{f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{{\mathbb{V}}_{0}})}{h({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})}\right)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln\left(\frac{f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{{\mathbb{V}}})}{h({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})}\right)\Biggr]+\bigl[\Xi_{({\mathbb{V}})}-\Xi_{({\mathbb{V}}_{0})}\bigr]\log N

(19)

To prove the theorem, we will prove that:

\forall\,{\mathbb{V}}\in{\mathbb{V}}_{1},\mathbb{P}[\Delta\mbox{BIC}({\mathbb{V}})<0]\underset{N\rightarrow\infty}{\rightarrow}0

Denoting
{\mathbb{M}}_{N}({\mathbb{V}})=\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln\left(\dfrac{f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{{\mathbb{V}}})}{h({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})}\right),M({\mathbb{V}})=-D_{\mathrm{KL}}[h,f(\cdot;{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{{\mathbb{V}}})]
, from
Equation

19
, we get:

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{P}(\Delta\mbox{BIC}({\mathbb{V}})<0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{P}(2N({\mathbb{M}}_{N}({\mathbb{V}}_{0})-{\mathbb{M}}_{N}({\mathbb{V}}))+\bigl[\Xi_{({\mathbb{V}})}-\Xi_{({\mathbb{V}}_{0})}\bigr]\log N<0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{P}({\mathbb{M}}_{N}({\mathbb{V}}_{0})-M({\mathbb{V}}_{0})+M({\mathbb{V}}_{0})-M({\mathbb{V}})+M({\mathbb{V}})-{\mathbb{M}}_{N}({\mathbb{V}})+\frac{\bigl[\Xi_{({\mathbb{V}})}-\Xi_{({\mathbb{V}}_{0})}\bigr]\log N}{2N}<0)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{P}({\mathbb{M}}_{N}({\mathbb{V}}_{0})-M({\mathbb{V}}_{0})>{\epsilon})+\mathbb{P}(M({\mathbb{V}})-{\mathbb{M}}_{N}({\mathbb{V}})>{\epsilon})+\mathbb{P}(M({\mathbb{V}}_{0})-M({\mathbb{V}})+\frac{\bigl[\Xi_{({\mathbb{V}})}-\Xi_{({\mathbb{V}}_{0})}\bigr]\log N}{2N}<2{\epsilon})

From Proposition
4
, we know that

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln\left(\frac{h({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o})}{f({\bm{y}}_{n}^{o};\hat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{{\mathbb{V}}})}\right)\underset{n\to\infty}{\overset{P}{\to}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}\Bigl[h,f(\cdot;{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{{\mathbb{V}}})\Bigr].

This leads to
{\mathbb{M}}_{N}({\mathbb{V}})\underset{n\to\infty}{\overset{P}{\to}}M({\mathbb{V}})
. Similar to the proof for the
{\mathbb{S}}{\mathbb{R}}{\mathbb{U}}{\mathbb{W}}
model in Maugis
[
38
]
, we prove the theorem.
∎

D.3
Proof of Theorem
3

Throughout this proof, we analyze the estimator defined by minimizing
the
negative log-likelihood
penalized. Accordingly, although we denote by

\ell({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})=\ln f({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})

the usual log-likelihood of a single observation, we consistently work with its negative
-\ell
as the optimization objective. To streamline notation, we therefore use the term “score” to mean the gradient of the
negative
log-likelihood:

S_{j}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}):=-\,\frac{\partial\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial\alpha_{j}}.

This convention differs by a minus sign from the standard statistical score,
but it ensures that all gradients, Hessians, and Fisher information matrices below are taken with respect to the minimized objective
-\ell_{N}
.

We start with presenting some useful lemmas before proving our main theorem. These lemmas establish fundamental properties of the penalized GMM estimators and the variable ranking procedure.

Lemma 1

(Score Function Components)
.

Let
f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})=\sum_{m=1}^{K}\pi_{m}\Phi({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\mu}}_{m},{\bm{\Sigma}}_{m})
be the p.d.f of a
K
-component GMM for an observation
{\bm{y}}_{n}
, where
{\bm{\alpha}}=({\bm{\pi}},\{{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K},\{{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K})
represents the GMM parameters. Let
{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}={\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{-1}
be the precision matrix for component
k
. The log-likelihood for observation
{\bm{y}}_{n}
is
\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})=\ln f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})
.
The responsibility for component
k
given observation
{\bm{y}}_{n}
and parameters
{\bm{\alpha}}
is
t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})=\frac{\pi_{k}\Phi({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\mu}}_{k},{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k})}{f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}
.
The score components,
S_{j}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})
, where
\alpha_{j}
is a parameter in
{\bm{\alpha}}
and
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
are the true parameter values, are given as follows:

1.

Mixing proportions
\pi_{k}
:

When parameterizing
\pi_{K}=1-\sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\pi_{j}
, the score component for
\pi_{k}
,
k\in\{1,\dots,K-1\}
, is:

S_{\pi_{k}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=\frac{t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})}{\pi_{K}^{*}}-\frac{t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})}{\pi_{k}^{*}}

2.

Mean parameters
({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}
:

Let
({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}
be the
d
-th component of the mean vector
{\bm{\mu}}_{k}
. The score component is:

S_{({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=-t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})({\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*}))_{d}

3.

Precision matrix parameters
({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}
:

Let
({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}
be the element
(r,s)
of the symmetric precision matrix
{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}
. The score component is:

S_{({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=-t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\frac{(2-\delta_{rs})}{2}\left(({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{*})_{rs}-({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{r}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{s}\right)

where
\delta_{rs}
is the Kronecker delta.

Proof of Lemma
1
.

Let
\Phi_{m}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})=\Phi({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\mu}}_{m},{\bm{\Sigma}}_{m})
. The log-likelihood for a single observation
{\bm{y}}_{n}
is
\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})=\ln\left(\sum_{m=1}^{K}\pi_{m}\Phi_{m}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})\right)
. The score component for a generic parameter
\alpha_{j}
is
S_{j}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=-\frac{\partial\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial\alpha_{j}}|_{{\bm{\alpha}}={\bm{\alpha}}^{*}}
.

Score components for mixing proportions
\pi_{k}
.

We parameterize
\pi_{K}=1-\sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\pi_{j}
. For
k\in\{1,\dots,K-1\}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial\pi_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}\frac{\partial}{\partial\pi_{k}}\left(\sum_{m=1}^{K-1}\pi_{m}\Phi_{m}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})+\left(1-\sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\pi_{j}\right)\Phi_{K}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Phi_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})-\Phi_{K}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}

Since
t_{m}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})=\frac{\pi_{m}\Phi_{m}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}
, we have
\frac{\Phi_{m}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}=\frac{t_{m}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\pi_{m}}
.
Thus,

\frac{\partial\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial\pi_{k}}=\frac{t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\pi_{k}}-\frac{t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\pi_{K}}

The score component at
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
is:

S_{\pi_{k}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=-\left(\frac{t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})}{\pi_{k}^{*}}-\frac{t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})}{\pi_{K}^{*}}\right)=\frac{t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})}{\pi_{K}^{*}}-\frac{t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})}{\pi_{k}^{*}}\,.

Score components for mean parameters
({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}
.
Let
({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}
be the
d
-th component of
{\bm{\mu}}_{k}
.

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}\frac{\partial}{\partial({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}\left(\sum_{m=1}^{K}\pi_{m}\Phi_{m}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi_{k}}{f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}\frac{\partial\Phi_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}

Since
\frac{\partial\Phi_{k}}{\partial({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}=\Phi_{k}\frac{\partial\ln\Phi_{k}}{\partial({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}
, and for
\Phi_{k}
,
\ln\Phi_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})=C_{k}-\frac{1}{2}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})^{\top}{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})
, we have:

\frac{\partial\ln\Phi_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}=({\bm{\Psi}}_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}))_{d}

Substituting this back:

\frac{\partial\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}=\frac{\pi_{k}\Phi_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}({\bm{\Psi}}_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}))_{d}=t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})({\bm{\Psi}}_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}))_{d}

The score component at
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
is:

S_{({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=-t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})({\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*}))_{d}\,.

Score components for precision matrix parameters
({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}
.

Let
({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}
be an element of the symmetric precision matrix
{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}
.

\frac{\partial\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}=\frac{\pi_{k}}{f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}\frac{\partial\Phi_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}=t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})\frac{1}{\pi_{k}}\pi_{k}\frac{\partial\ln\Phi_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}

The log-density of a single Gaussian component is
\ln\Phi_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})=C^{\prime}_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\ln\det({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})-\frac{1}{2}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})^{\top}{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})
.
For a symmetric matrix
{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}
, the derivative with respect to an element
({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}
(using the “symmetric” derivative convention where
\frac{\partial}{\partial X_{rs}}
means varying
X_{rs}
and
X_{sr}
simultaneously if
r\neq s
) is:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\ln\det({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})}{\partial({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}

\displaystyle=(2-\delta_{rs})({\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{-1})_{rs}=(2-\delta_{rs})({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k})_{rs}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})^{\top}{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})}{\partial({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}

\displaystyle=(2-\delta_{rs})({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{r}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{s}

Therefore,

\frac{\partial\ln\Phi_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}=\frac{1}{2}(2-\delta_{rs})({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k})_{rs}-\frac{1}{2}(2-\delta_{rs})({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{r}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{s}

So,

\frac{\partial\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})}{\partial({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}=t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})\frac{(2-\delta_{rs})}{2}\left(({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k})_{rs}-({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{r}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{s}\right)

The score component at
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
is:

S_{({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=-t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\frac{(2-\delta_{rs})}{2}\left(({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{*})_{rs}-({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{r}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{s}\right)\,.

∎

For the two lemmas below, we will use the following assumptions:

Assumption 7

(Identifiability & Smoothness)
.

The GMM density
f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})
is identifiable.
\ell_{1}({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})
. is three times continuously differentiable w.r.t.
{\bm{\alpha}}
in an open ball
{\mathbb{B}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*},r_{0})
around
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
.

Assumption 8

(Compact Parameter Space & True Parameter Properties)
.

{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
is an interior point of a compact set
{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{V}}}\subset{\mathbb{B}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*},r_{0})
. This implies:

•

Mixing proportions:
\pi_{k}^{*}\geq\pi_{\min}>0
for all
k=1,\dots,K
, for some constant
\pi_{\min}\in(0,1/K]
.

•

Means:
\|{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*}\|_{2}\leq\eta<\infty
for all
k
.

•

Covariance and Precision Matrices: The covariance matrices
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{*}
have eigenvalues
\lambda({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{*})
such that
0<s_{m}\leq\lambda({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{*})\leq s_{M}<\infty
.
Consequently, for the precision matrices
{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}=({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{*})^{-1}
, their eigenvalues
\lambda({\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*})
satisfy
0<1/s_{M}\leq\lambda({\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*})\leq 1/s_{m}<\infty
.
We define
\sigma_{\min}^{2}=s_{m}
,
\sigma_{\max}^{2}=s_{M}
.
And for precision matrices,
\theta_{\min}=1/s_{M}
,
\theta_{\max}=1/s_{m}
.

All
{\bm{\alpha}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{V}}}
satisfy these bounds. Let
L_{3}
be an upper bound on the norm of the third derivative tensor of
\ell_{1}({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})
for
{\bm{\alpha}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{V}}}
, such that
\mathbb{E}[L_{3}({\bm{y}})]<\infty
.

Assumption 9

(Data Distribution)
.

Observations
{\bm{y}}_{1},\dots,{\bm{y}}_{N}
are i.i.d. from
f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})
. For each
{\bm{y}}_{n}
, there exists a latent class variable
{\textnormal{z}}_{n}\in\{1,\dots,K\}
with
P({\textnormal{z}}_{n}=k)=\pi_{k}^{*}
, such that
{\bm{y}}_{n}|{\textnormal{z}}_{n}=k\sim\mathcal{N}({\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*},{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{*})
.

Assumption 10

(Bounded Posteriors)
.

For
{\bm{\alpha}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{V}}}
, the posterior probabilities
t_{k}({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})=\frac{\pi_{k}\phi({\bm{y}}|{\bm{\mu}}_{k},{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k})}{\sum_{j=1}^{K}\pi_{j}\phi({\bm{y}}|{\bm{\mu}}_{j},{\bm{\Sigma}}_{j})}
satisfy
0<t_{k}({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})\leq 1
.

The empirical Hessian is
{\bm{H}}_{N}({\bm{\alpha}})=\nabla^{2}\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}})
. The Fisher Information Matrix is
{\bm{I}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}}[\nabla^{2}\ell({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})]
. Moreover, we have the following definition of the true support of penalized parameters:

Definition 3

(True Support of Penalized Parameters
{\mathbb{S}}_{0}
)
.

Let
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}=({\bm{\pi}}^{*},\{{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*}\}_{k=1}^{K},\{{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}\}_{k=1}^{K})
be the true GMM parameter vector.
Consider the penalty

P({\bm{\alpha}})=\lambda\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\|_{1}+\rho\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}\|_{1}.

We define the true support set
{\mathbb{S}}_{0}
as the collection of indices corresponding to nonzero parameters in
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
that are subject to penalization:

{\mathbb{S}}_{0}={\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\mu}}}^{*}\cup{\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\Psi}}}^{*},\qquad s_{0}=|{\mathbb{S}}_{0}|=s_{{\bm{\mu}}}^{*}+s_{{\bm{\Psi}}}^{*}.

Here:

1.

Support of means

{\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\mu}}}^{*}=\big\{(k,d):1\leq k\leq K,\;1\leq d\leq p,\;({\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{d}\neq 0\big\},\quad s_{{\bm{\mu}}}^{*}=|{\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\mu}}}^{*}|.

2.

Support of precision matrices

{\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\Psi}}}^{*}=\big\{(k,r,s):1\leq k\leq K,\;1\leq r,s\leq p,\;({\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*})_{rs}\neq 0\big\},\quad s_{{\bm{\Psi}}}^{*}=|{\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\Psi}}}^{*}|.

Assumption 11

(Restricted Eigenvalue Condition)
.

For any parameter increment vector
{\bm{\Delta}}
indexed compatibly with
{\bm{\alpha}}
, we
{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}
for its restriction to indices in
{\mathbb{S}}_{0}
and
{\bm{\Delta}}_{({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}
for its complement. For
c_{0}\geq 1
, define the cone

{\mathcal{C}}(c_{0},{\mathbb{S}}_{0})=\big\{{\bm{\Delta}}:\lVert{\bm{\Delta}}_{({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}\rVert_{1}\leq c_{0}\lVert{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\rVert_{1}\big\}.

Assume the FIM
{\bm{I}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})
is positive definite, then there exists
\kappa_{I}>0
such that, for all
{\bm{\Delta}}\in{\mathcal{C}}(c_{0},{\mathbb{S}}_{0})
,

{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}{\bm{I}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}){\bm{\Delta}}\geq\kappa_{I}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Remark 1
.

The constant
c_{0}
is chosen to match the cone where the estimation error
\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
lies.
A sufficient choice is
c_{0}\geq\frac{A_{0}+1}{A_{0}-1}
when the regularization level
satisfies
\lambda\geq A_{0}\,\|\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\|_{\infty}
with
A_{0}>1
.

Assumption 12

(Uniform Hessian Concentration)
.

et
d_{\alpha}=(K-1)+KD+KD(D+1)/2
denote the number of free parameters in
{\bm{\alpha}}
. There exist constants
C_{H}>0
,
c_{1}^{H},c_{2}^{H}>0
and a radius
\delta_{R}>0
such that,
for
N\gtrsim s_{0}\ln d_{\alpha}
, with probability at least
1-c_{1}^{H}d_{\alpha}^{-c_{2}^{H}}
,

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}\in{\mathbb{B}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*},\delta_{R})\cap{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{V}}}\\
{\bm{\Delta}}\in{\mathcal{C}}(c_{0},{\mathbb{S}}_{0}),\ \|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}=1\end{subarray}}\Big|\,{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}\big({\bm{H}}_{N}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}})-{\bm{I}}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}})\big){\bm{\Delta}}\,\Big|\;\leq\;C_{H}\sqrt{\frac{s_{0}\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}.

The constant
C_{H}
depends on the bounds in Assumption
8

and on moment bounds for derivatives of
\ell
.
The radius can be taken as
\delta_{R}\asymp\sqrt{s_{0}\ln d_{\alpha}/N}
.

Lemma 2

(Gradient Bound)
.

Let
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}=({\bm{\pi}}^{*},{\bm{\mu}}_{1}^{*},\dots,{\bm{\mu}}_{K}^{*},{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}^{*},\dots,{\bm{\Psi}}_{K}^{*})
be the true parameter vector of a
K
-component GMM with
D
-dimensional components.
Suppose Assumptions
7
-
10
hold.
Let
d_{\alpha}
be defined in Assumption
12
. Then there exist absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
and
C_{g}>0
such that, for any
N\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\|\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\|_{\infty}\leq C_{g}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}\right)\;\geq\;1-C_{1}\,d_{\alpha}^{-C_{2}},

where
\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})
is the empirical
negative
log-likelihood (recall
\ell({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})=-\ln f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})
). An explicit choice is
C_{g}=\sqrt{2(C_{2}+1)/c_{0}}\,\nu_{\max}
, where
c_{0}
is an absolute constant in Bernstein’s inequality (e.g.,
c_{0}=1/2
).
This bound holds provided
N\geq C_{B}\ln d_{\alpha}
, with

C_{B}\;=\;\frac{2(C_{2}+1)\,\nu_{\max}^{2}}{c_{0}\big(\min_{j:\,b_{j}\neq 0}(\mathbb{E}[S_{jn}^{2}]/b_{j})\big)^{2}},

\nu_{\max}^{2}=\max_{j}\mathbb{E}[S_{jn}^{2}]
, and
b_{j}
is the sub-exponential scale of
S_{jn}
(
b_{j}=0
for sub-Gaussian components).

Proof of Lemma
2
.

By Assumptions
7
and
9
(regularity and correct specification),

\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}}[S_{j}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})]=0\quad\text{ for all }j=1,\dots,d_{\alpha}

Let
\nu_{j}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[S_{j}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})^{2}]
be the variance of the score evaluated at
{\alpha}^{*}_{j}
. We bound variances and tail parameters for each parameter block.

Mixing proportions
S_{\pi_{k}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})
.

By Lemma
1
,

S_{\pi_{k}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=\frac{t_{K}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})}{\pi_{K}^{*}}-\frac{t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})}{\pi_{k}^{*}}.

By Assumption
10
,
0<t_{m}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\leq 1
, and by Assumption
8
,
\pi_{m}^{*}\geq\pi_{\min}>0
; hence

|S_{\pi_{k}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})|\leq 2/\pi_{\min}
. Thus
S_{\pi_{k}}
is bounded sub-Gaussian with

\nu_{\pi_{k}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[S_{\pi_{k}}^{2}]\leq(2/\pi_{\min})^{2}
and
b_{\pi_{k}}=0
.

Mean parameters
S_{({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})
.

From Lemma
1
,

S_{({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=-\,t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\,\big({\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})\big)_{d}.

Let
U_{nkd}:=\big({\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})\big)_{d}
.
Conditionally on
z_{n}=\ell
, we have
{\bm{y}}_{n}={\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}+\varepsilon_{\ell}
with
\varepsilon_{\ell}\sim\mathcal{N}({\bm{0}},{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\ell}^{*})
.
Then

\mathrm{Var}\big(U_{nkd}\mid z_{n}=\ell\big)=e_{d}^{\top}{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\ell}^{*}{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}e_{d}\;\leq\;\|{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\,\|{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\ell}^{*}\|_{2}\;\leq\;\theta_{\max}^{2}\,\sigma_{\max}^{2},

and

\big|\mathbb{E}[U_{nkd}\mid z_{n}=\ell]\big|=\big|e_{d}^{\top}{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}({\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})\big|\;\leq\;\|{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}\|_{2}\,\|{\bm{\mu}}_{\ell}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*}\|_{2}\;\leq\;\theta_{\max}\,(2\eta).

Hence
\mathbb{E}[U_{nkd}^{2}]\leq\theta_{\max}^{2}(\sigma_{\max}^{2}+4\eta^{2})
.
Since
|t_{k}|\leq 1
,

\nu_{({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\big[S_{({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}^{2}\big]\;\leq\;\mathbb{E}[U_{nkd}^{2}]\;\leq\;\theta_{\max}^{2}(\sigma_{\max}^{2}+4\eta^{2}).

Moreover,
U_{nkd}
is (conditionally) Gaussian and hence sub-Gaussian; with the bounded multiplier
t_{k}
,
S_{({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}
is sub-Gaussian, so
b_{({\bm{\mu}}_{k})_{d}}=0
.

Precision entries
S_{({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})
.

From Lemma
1
,

S_{({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=-\,t_{k}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\,\frac{(2-\delta_{rs})}{2}\,\Big(({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{*})_{rs}-({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{r}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{s}\Big).

Let
V_{nkrs}:=({\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{*})_{rs}-({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{r}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{s}
.
Each
({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{r}
is sub-Gaussian with parameter
\lesssim\sigma_{\max}+\eta
, so the product

({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{r}({\bm{y}}_{n}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*})_{s}
is sub-exponential; hence
V_{nkrs}
is sub-exponential.
Since
|t_{k}|\leq 1
,
S_{({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}
is sub-exponential.
Thus there exist constants
C_{\Psi,\nu},C_{\Psi,b}>0
such that

\nu_{({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[S_{({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}^{2}\right]\;\leq\;C_{\Psi,\nu}\,(\sigma_{\max}^{2}+\eta^{2})^{2},\qquad b_{({\bm{\Psi}}_{k})_{rs}}\;\leq\;C_{\Psi,b}\,(\sigma_{\max}^{2}+\eta^{2}).

Union bound.

Let
\nu_{\max}^{2}=\max_{j}\mathbb{E}[S_{jn}^{2}]
and
b_{\max}=\max_{j}\{b_{j}:b_{j}\neq 0\}
; by the above bounds these depend only on
(\pi_{\min},\eta,s_{m},s_{M})
.
By Bernstein’s inequality, for each coordinate
j
and any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}S_{jn}\right|\geq t\right)\;\leq\;2\exp\left(-c_{0}N\min\left(\frac{t^{2}}{\nu_{j}^{2}},\,\frac{t}{b_{j}}\right)\right),

with the convention that if
b_{j}=0
then
\min(\cdot)=t^{2}/\nu_{j}^{2}
.
Set
t_{N}=C_{g}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}
and assume
N\geq C_{B}\ln d_{\alpha}
so that

t_{N}\leq\min_{j:b_{j}\neq 0}\nu_{j}^{2}/b_{j}
. Then for all
j
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}S_{jn}\right|\geq t_{N}\right)\;\leq\;2\exp\left(-\frac{c_{0}Nt_{N}^{2}}{2\nu_{j}^{2}}\right)\;\leq\;2\exp\left(-\frac{c_{0}Nt_{N}^{2}}{2\nu_{\max}^{2}}\right)=2\,d_{\alpha}^{-\frac{c_{0}C_{g}^{2}}{2\nu_{\max}^{2}}}.

A union bound over
j=1,\dots,d_{\alpha}
yields

\mathbb{P}\left(\|\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\|_{\infty}\geq t_{N}\right)\;\leq\;2\,d_{\alpha}^{\,1-\frac{c_{0}C_{g}^{2}}{2\nu_{\max}^{2}}}.

Choosing
C_{1}=2
and
C_{g}
so that
\frac{c_{0}C_{g}^{2}}{2\nu_{\max}^{2}}=C_{2}+1
gives the claim, i.e.,

C_{g}=\sqrt{2(C_{2}+1)/c_{0}}\,\nu_{\max}
.
Finally, the condition
N\geq C_{B}\ln d_{\alpha}
is guaranteed by taking

C_{B}\;=\;\frac{C_{g}^{2}}{\big(\min_{j:\,b_{j}\neq 0}\nu_{j}^{2}/b_{j}\big)^{2}}\;=\;\frac{2(C_{2}+1)\,\nu_{\max}^{2}}{c_{0}\big(\min_{j:\,b_{j}\neq 0}(\mathbb{E}[S_{jn}^{2}]/b_{j})\big)^{2}}.

∎

Lemma 3

(Parameter Consistency for Penalized GMM Estimator)
.

Let
\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}
be any local minimizer of the penalized negative average log-likelihood

Q({\bm{\alpha}})=\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}})+P({\bm{\alpha}}),

where

\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}})=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ln f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}}),\qquad P({\bm{\alpha}})=\lambda\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\|_{1}+\rho\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}\|_{1}.

Let
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
be the true GMM parameter vector, and
{\mathbb{S}}_{0}
be the true support of the penalized parameters in
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
, with sparsity
s_{0}=|{\mathbb{S}}_{0}|
.
Let
d_{\alpha}
be the total number of free parameters in
{\bm{\alpha}}
as defined in Assumptions
12
.

Suppose Assumptions
7
-
12
hold and the gradient bound in Lemma
2
is satisfied.
Choose the regularization parameters
\lambda=\rho=\lambda_{\text{chosen}}
, where

\lambda_{\text{chosen}}=A_{0}C_{g}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}

for a constant
A_{0}>1
(e.g.
A_{0}=3
). Then, provided
N\gtrsim s_{0}\ln d_{\alpha}
, with probability at least

1-C_{1}^{\text{grad}}d_{\alpha}^{-C_{2}^{\text{grad}}}-c_{1}^{H}d_{\alpha}^{-c_{2}^{H}}
:

1.

(
L_{2}
-norm consistency):

\|\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}\|_{2}\leq\frac{2(A_{0}+1)C_{g}}{\kappa_{I}}\sqrt{\frac{s_{0}\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}.

2.

(
L_{1}
-norm consistency):

\|\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}\|_{1}\leq\frac{4A_{0}(A_{0}+1)C_{g}}{(A_{0}-1)\kappa_{I}}\,s_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}.

Here
C_{g}
is the gradient bound constant from Lemma
2
, and
\kappa_{I}
is the restricted eigenvalue constant from Assumption
11
.
The factor
A_{0}
is a user-chosen constant controlling the regularization strength.

Proof of Lemma
3
.

Let
\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}
be a minimizer of the penalized negative log-likelihood

Q({\bm{\alpha}})=\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}})+P({\bm{\alpha}})
, where

P({\bm{\alpha}})=\lambda\sum_{k}\|{\bm{\mu}}_{k}\|_{1}+\rho\sum_{k}\|{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}\|_{1}
and

\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\ell({\bm{y}}_{n};{\bm{\alpha}})
with

\ell({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})=-\ln f_{\text{clust}}({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})
.
Since
\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}
minimizes
Q
, we have

\ell_{N}(\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}})-\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\;\leq\;P({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})-P(\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}).

(20)

Let
{\bm{\Delta}}=\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
and assume
\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{V}}}
so that

{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}+{\bm{\Delta}}\in{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{V}}}
. A second-order Taylor expansion gives

\ell_{N}(\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}})-\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})=\langle\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}),{\bm{\Delta}}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\,{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}+t_{0}{\bm{\Delta}})\,{\bm{\Delta}}

for some
t_{0}\in(0,1)
. Write
\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}={\bm{\alpha}}^{*}+t_{0}{\bm{\Delta}}
. We lower bound

\frac{1}{2}{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{N}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}){\bm{\Delta}}
by decomposing

\displaystyle{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{N}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}){\bm{\Delta}}

\displaystyle={\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}{\bm{I}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}){\bm{\Delta}}+{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}\big({\bm{I}}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}})-{\bm{I}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\big){\bm{\Delta}}+{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}\big({\bm{H}}_{N}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}})-{\bm{I}}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}})\big){\bm{\Delta}}.

From this point, all bounds are stated for arbitrary
{\bm{\Delta}}
in the cone
{\mathcal{C}}(c_{0},{\mathbb{S}}_{0})
;
the fact that the actual error

\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
lies in
{\mathcal{C}}(c_{0},{\mathbb{S}}_{0})
will be established later by Lemma
4
.

By Assumption
11
, for
{\bm{\Delta}}\in{\mathcal{C}}(c_{0},{\mathbb{S}}_{0})
,

{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}{\bm{I}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}){\bm{\Delta}}\;\geq\;\kappa_{I}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}.

By Assumption
7
and compactness (Assumption
8
),

{\bm{\alpha}}\mapsto{\bm{I}}({\bm{\alpha}})=\mathbb{E}[\nabla^{2}\ell({\bm{y}};{\bm{\alpha}})]
is Lipschitz on

{\mathbb{B}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*},r_{0})\cap{\bm{\Theta}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbb{V}}}
in spectral norm: there exists
L_{I}>0
such that

\|{\bm{I}}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}})-{\bm{I}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\|_{2}\;\leq\;L_{I}\|\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}-{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}\|_{2}=L_{I}t_{0}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}\;\leq\;L_{I}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2},

and therefore

\big|{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}({\bm{I}}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}})-{\bm{I}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})){\bm{\Delta}}\big|\;\leq\;\|{\bm{I}}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}})-{\bm{I}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\|_{2}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}\;\leq\;L_{I}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{3}\;\leq\;L_{I}\delta_{R}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2},

provided
\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{R}
.
By Assumption
12
, if
\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{R}
and
{\bm{\Delta}}\in{\mathcal{C}}(c_{0},{\mathbb{S}}_{0})
, then with probability at least

1-c_{1}^{H}d_{\alpha}^{-c_{2}^{H}}
,

\big|{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}({\bm{H}}_{N}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}})-{\bm{I}}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}})){\bm{\Delta}}\big|\;\leq\;C_{H}\sqrt{\frac{s_{0}\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Combining the three representations, for
{\bm{\Delta}}\in{\mathcal{C}}(c_{0},{\mathbb{S}}_{0})
with
\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{R}
,

\frac{1}{2}{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{N}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}){\bm{\Delta}}\;\geq\;\frac{1}{2}\Big(\kappa_{I}-L_{I}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}-C_{H}\sqrt{\frac{s_{0}\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}\Big)\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Choose
\delta_{R}
and
N
so that
L_{I}\delta_{R}\leq\kappa_{I}/4
and

C_{H}\sqrt{\frac{s_{0}\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}\leq\kappa_{I}/4
(e.g.,
N\gtrsim(C_{H}^{2}/\kappa_{I}^{2})s_{0}\ln d_{\alpha}
).
Then, with the same probability,

\frac{1}{2}{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{N}(\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}){\bm{\Delta}}\;\geq\;\frac{\kappa_{I}}{4}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}.

Hence we obtain the
restricted strong convexity
(RSC) inequality on the cone:

\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}+{\bm{\Delta}})-\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})-\langle\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}),{\bm{\Delta}}\rangle\;\geq\;\frac{\kappa_{L}}{2}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2},\qquad\text{for all }{\bm{\Delta}}\in{\mathcal{C}}(c_{0},{\mathbb{S}}_{0}),\ \|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{R},

(21)

where
\kappa_{L}=\kappa_{I}/2>0
.

Stochastic term.

By Lemma
2
, if
N\geq C_{B}\ln d_{\alpha}
, then with probability at least

1-C_{1}^{\text{grad}}d_{\alpha}^{-C_{2}^{\text{grad}}}
,

\|\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\|_{\infty}\;\leq\;C_{g}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}.

By Hölder’s inequality
\Big(\langle x,y\rangle<=\lVert x\rVert_{\infty}\lVert y\rVert_{1}\Big)
,

|\langle\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}),{\bm{\Delta}}\rangle|\;\leq\;\|\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\|_{\infty}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{1}\;\leq\;C_{g}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{1}.

(22)

Penalty difference.

Let
{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\mu}}_{k}}=\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}_{k}-{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*}
and
{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}}=\widehat{{\bm{\Psi}}}_{k}-{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}
, and denote
by
{\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\mu}}}^{*}
and
{\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\Psi}}}^{*}
the true supports (Definition
3
).
Using the
L_{1}
-triangle inequality on supports: for any vector
{\bm{a}},{\bm{b}}
and support
{\mathbb{S}}
of
{\bm{a}}
:
\|{\bm{a}}\|_{1}-\|{\bm{b}}\|_{1}\leq\|{\bm{a}}_{{\mathbb{S}}}-{\bm{b}}_{{\mathbb{S}}}\|_{1}-\|{\bm{a}}_{({\mathbb{S}})^{c}}-{\bm{b}}_{({\mathbb{S}})^{c}}\|_{1}+2\|{\bm{a}}_{({\mathbb{S}})^{c}}\|_{1}
. Since
{\bm{a}}_{({\mathbb{S}})^{c}}={\bm{0}}
:

\|{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{*}\|_{1}-\|\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}_{k}\|_{1}\;\leq\;\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\mu}}_{k},{\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\mu}}}^{*}}\|_{1}-\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\mu}}_{k},({\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\mu}}}^{*})^{c}}\|_{1},\qquad\|{\bm{\Psi}}_{k}^{*}\|_{1}-\|\widehat{{\bm{\Psi}}}_{k}\|_{1}\;\leq\;\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\Psi}}_{k},{\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\Psi}}}^{*}}\|_{1}-\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\Psi}}_{k},({\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\Psi}}}^{*})^{c}}\|_{1}.

Summing over
k
and writing
{\mathbb{S}}_{0}={\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\mu}}}^{*}\cup{\mathbb{S}}_{{\bm{\Psi}}}^{*}
,

\displaystyle P({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})-P(\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}})

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\big(\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\mu}},{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}-\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\mu}},({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}\|_{1}\big)+\rho\big(\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\Psi}},{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}-\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\Psi}},({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}\|_{1}\big).

(23)

Combining.

With probability at least
1-C_{1}^{\text{grad}}d_{\alpha}^{-C_{2}^{\text{grad}}}-c_{1}^{H}d_{\alpha}^{-c_{2}^{H}}
,
combining
Equation

21
,
Equation

22
, and
Equation

23
with
Equation

20
yields,
for all
{\bm{\Delta}}\in{\mathcal{C}}(c_{0},{\mathbb{S}}_{0})
with
\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{R}
,

\frac{\kappa_{L}}{2}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}\;\leq\;C_{g}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{1}+\lambda\big(\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\mu}},{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}-\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\mu}},({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}\|_{1}\big)+\rho\big(\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\Psi}},{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}-\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\bm{\Psi}},({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}\|_{1}\big).

(24)

Invoking the inequality
Equation

29
from Lemma
4
(the cone lemma, proved via KKT and the gradient bound, thus independent of RSC),

\frac{\kappa_{L}}{2}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{A_{0}}\right)\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\lambda_{j}|\Delta_{j}|-\left(1-\frac{1}{A_{0}}\right)\sum_{j\in({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}\lambda_{j}|\Delta_{j}|.

Since the second term is non-positive (as
A_{0}>1
and
\lambda_{j}|\Delta_{j}|\geq 0
), we can drop it to get an upper bound:

\frac{\kappa_{L}}{2}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{A_{0}}\right)\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\lambda_{j}|\Delta_{j}|.

Taking the common regularization level
\lambda_{\text{chosen}}=\lambda=\rho=A_{0}C_{g}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}
with
A_{0}>1
and note that
\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\lambda_{j}|\Delta_{j}|=\lambda_{\text{chosen}}\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}
, we obtain

\frac{\kappa_{L}}{2}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}\;\leq\;\Big(1+\frac{1}{A_{0}}\Big)\lambda_{\text{chosen}}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{s_{0}}\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{2}
, and since
\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{2}\leq\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}
:

\frac{\kappa_{L}}{2}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\lambda_{\text{chosen}}\left(1+\frac{1}{A_{0}}\right)\sqrt{s_{0}}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}.

If
\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}\neq 0
, we can divide by
\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}
:

\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}\leq\frac{2\lambda_{\text{chosen}}}{\kappa_{L}}\left(1+\frac{1}{A_{0}}\right)\sqrt{s_{0}}.

Substituting
\lambda_{\text{chosen}}=A_{0}C_{g}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}
:

\displaystyle\|\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2A_{0}C_{g}}{\kappa_{L}}\left(1+\frac{1}{A_{0}}\right)\sqrt{s_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(A_{0}+1)C_{g}}{\kappa_{L}}\sqrt{\frac{s_{0}\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}.

This proves the
L_{2}
-rate with constant
C_{L2}=\frac{2(A_{0}+1)C_{g}}{\kappa_{L}}
.

For the
L_{1}
-rate, Lemma
4
yields the cone bound

\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}\|_{1}\leq C_{\text{cone}}\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}
with

C_{\text{cone}}=\frac{A_{0}+1}{A_{0}-1}
(decomposability via
[
44
]
). Hence, assuming all
\lambda_{j}
for penalized components are
\lambda_{\text{chosen}}
):

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}+\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}+C_{\text{cone}}\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}=(1+C_{\text{cone}})\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{\text{cone}})\sqrt{s_{0}}\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{2}\quad(\text{by Cauchy-Schwarz})

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{\text{cone}})\sqrt{s_{0}}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}.

Substituting
C_{\text{cone}}=\frac{A_{0}+1}{A_{0}-1}
:

1+C_{\text{cone}}=1+\frac{A_{0}+1}{A_{0}-1}=\frac{A_{0}-1+A_{0}+1}{A_{0}-1}=\frac{2A_{0}}{A_{0}-1}.

So,

\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{1}\leq\left(\frac{2A_{0}}{A_{0}-1}\right)\sqrt{s_{0}}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}.

Now substitute the bound for
\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}
:

\displaystyle\|\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2A_{0}}{A_{0}-1}\right)\sqrt{s_{0}}\left(\frac{2(A_{0}+1)C_{g}}{\kappa_{L}}\sqrt{\frac{s_{0}\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{4A_{0}(A_{0}+1)C_{g}}{(A_{0}-1)\kappa_{L}}s_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}.

\displaystyle=C_{L1}(A_{0},C_{g},\kappa_{L})s_{0}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}

where
C_{L1}(A_{0},C_{g},\kappa_{L})=\frac{4A_{0}(A_{0}+1)C_{g}}{(A_{0}-1)\kappa_{L}}
. This concludes the proof of parameter consistency in
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
norms.
∎

Remark 2
.

The radius
\delta_{R}
governing the RSC inequality
Equation

21
is critical and is typically of the same order as the target statistical error. If
\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}
exceeds
\delta_{R}
, the Lipschitz remainder
L_{I}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{3}
can dominate, and/or the uniform Hessian concentration in Assumption
12
may fail on such a large neighborhood of
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
. In general M-estimation analyses (e.g.,
[
44
]
), one often proves an RSC with a tolerance term,

\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}+{\bm{\Delta}})-\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})-\langle\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}),{\bm{\Delta}}\rangle\;\geq\;\frac{\kappa_{L}}{2}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2}\;-\;\tau_{L}\,\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}\,\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{1}^{2},

valid on a larger set. Under Assumption
12
, we obtain sufficiently strong control to dispense with this tolerance and derive the cleaner quadratic curvature bound
Equation

21
. If Assumption
12
were weakened (e.g., only yielding a bound of the form

C_{H}\sqrt{\frac{\ln d_{\alpha}}{N}}\,\frac{\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{s_{0}}}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}
on
{\bm{\Delta}}^{\top}({\bm{H}}_{N}-{\bm{I}}){\bm{\Delta}}
over the cone), then a tolerance term proportional to
\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{1}^{2}
would naturally appear in the RSC.

Lemma 4

(Cone Condition for
L_{1}
-Penalized M-Estimators)
.

Let
\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}
be any local minimizer of
Q({\bm{\alpha}})=\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}})+P({\bm{\alpha}})
, where
\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}})
is a differentiable loss function and the penalty
P({\bm{\alpha}})
is a sum of component-wise
L_{1}
penalties:

P({\bm{\alpha}})=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\alpha}}\lambda_{j}|\alpha_{j}|.

Let
{\mathbb{S}}_{0}
be the true support of
{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
. Let
{\bm{\Delta}}=\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-{\bm{\alpha}}^{*}
.
Suppose the regularization parameters are chosen such that for some constant
A_{0}>1
:

\lambda_{j}\geq A_{0}|[\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})]_{j}|\quad\text{for all }j\in({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}.

(25)

and
\lambda_{j}
of similar order for
j\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}
. For simplicity, we often set
\lambda_{j}=\lambda_{\text{chosen}}
for all penalized components, where
\lambda_{\text{chosen}}\geq A_{0}\|\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\|_{\infty}
.
If the RSC condition from
Equation

21
holds for
{\bm{\Delta}}
:

\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}+{\bm{\Delta}})-\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})-\langle\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*}),{\bm{\Delta}}\rangle\geq\frac{\kappa_{L}}{2}\|{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{2}^{2},

then, for
A_{0}>1
, the error vector
{\bm{\Delta}}
satisfies the cone condition:

\sum_{j\in({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}\lambda_{j}|\Delta_{j}|\leq\frac{A_{0}+1}{A_{0}-1}\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\lambda_{j}|\Delta_{j}|.

(26)

If all
\lambda_{j}
for penalized components are equal to
\lambda_{\text{chosen}}
, this simplifies to:

\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}\|_{\text{pen},1}\leq\frac{A_{0}+1}{A_{0}-1}\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{\text{pen},1},

where
\|\cdot\|_{\text{pen},1}
refers to the
L_{1}
norm over the components that are actually penalized. If all components were penalized with the same
\lambda_{0}
, this would be
\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{({\mathbb{S}}_{0})^{c}}\|_{1}\leq\frac{A_{0}+1}{A_{0}-1}\|{\bm{\Delta}}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|_{1}
.

Remark 3
.

In our case,
\lambda_{j}=\lambda
for mean components
{\mu}_{kd}
, and
\lambda_{j}=\rho
for off-diagonal precision components
({\Psi}_{k})_{rs}
, and
\lambda_{j}=0
for parameters not penalized like proportions or diagonal precision elements if they are not penalized towards a specific value. Moreover, the Cone Condition ensures that the error vector is primarily concentrated on the true support. The key is that the regularization parameter for the "noise" variables (off-support) must be sufficiently larger than the corresponding component of the score vector, allowing the penalty to effectively shrink noise components.

Proof of Lemma
4
.

Let
\mathcal{P}\subset\{1,\dots,d_{\alpha}\}
denote the index set of
penalized
coordinates and write

\|{\bm{x}}\|_{\text{pen},1}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{P}}|x_{j}|
. In what follows, sums and
L_{1}
-norms are taken over
\mathcal{P}
.
Assume the tuning condition holds on
all penalized
coordinates:

\lambda_{j}\;\geq\;A_{0}\,\big|[\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})]_{j}\big|\qquad\forall\,j\in\mathcal{P},\quad A_{0}>1,

(27)

equivalently
\lambda_{\text{chosen}}\geq A_{0}\|\nabla\ell_{N}({\bm{\alpha}}^{*})\|_{\infty,\text{pen}}
when a common level is used.

2
2

2

If some coordinates are unpenalized, they are excluded from
\mathcal{P}
and do not enter the cone inequality.

Since
\widehat{{\bm{\alpha}}}
is a local minimizer of
Q({\bm{\alpha}})
